# Hari-nāma-cintāmaṇi 4

## Foreword

*by Bhaktivinoda Thakur*

This book is not meant for everyone. It has been written for those who have developed a strong faith in Lord Chaitanya and in the practice of devotional service through taking shelter of the Holy Name. Lord Sri Chaitanya Mahaprabhu teaches in His *Śikṣāṣṭaka* that there are many types of devotional practice, but only through chanting the Holy Names can one attain all perfections. Those who believe in the truth of this teaching stand above all other practitioners of spiritual life.

Lord Chaitanya appointed His devotee Hari Das Thakur to teach this doctrine. According to authoritative sources, Hari Das was born in a Muslim family in the village of Burhan near Bongaon. Even so, the spiritual culture of his previous lives revived in him the desire to worship Lord Krishna while he was still young. As a result, he left home and built a cottage in the woods near Benapole where he took complete shelter of the Holy Name and meditation on the Supreme Lord. Unfortunately, he was obliged to leave Benapole when people who could not understand his spiritual vocation began to disturb him. These wicked people tried to disrupt his practice and destroy his reputation by sending a prostitute to tempt him, but this woman was converted by Hari Das when she heard the Holy Name from his saintly mouth. The blessed saint gave her his cottage and set off toward the Ganges while she took up a life of spiritual dedication.

With the Holy Names on his lips, Hari Das crossed the Ganges and came to Saptagram where he stayed as the guest of of Yadunandan Acharya, the spiritual master of two wealthy brothers, Hiranya and Govardhan Majumdar. Yadunandan regularly took Hari Das to religious meetings held at the Majumdar house. Govardhan’s young son Raghunath Das often came to see him and became one of Chaitanya Mahaprabhu’s greatest devotees as a result of Hari Das’ blessings.

At one of these meetings in the Majumdar household, Hari Das was drawn into a protracted debate on the nature of the Holy Name with a so-called Brahmin who worked for the Majumdar brothers named Gopal Chakravarti. Hiranya and Govardhan fired Gopal when he became offensive to Hari Das in the course of this debate, but the unfortunate man had to suffer graver consequences when he was subsequently afflicted with leprosy.

When Hari Das learned of Gopal Chakravarti’s suffering, he felt a great deal of sadness and left Saptagram to live in a cave in Phuliya, a village not far from the residence of Advaita Acharya in Shantipur. Under the Acharya’s guidance, Hari Das engaged in constant worship of the Lord. Though he did so in seclusion, the nature of pure devotion is such that, much as a devotee avoids the limelight, his glories eventually become known and attract sincere seekers of spiritual life. When Hari Das’ brilliant reputation began to spread, many local Muslims became jealous and approached the governor to have him punished for converting to Hinduism. Though Hari Das was subsequently tortured, his heart was so full of compassion that he never condemned his attackers, but only wished the best for them. Once freed, he returned to his cave in Phuliya.

Not long afterwards, Lord Chaitanya appeared in Nabadwip and Hari Das went with Advaita Prabhu to take shelter of His lotus feet. Thenceforth he acted as the Lord’s special lieutenant for preaching the glories of the Holy Name. When the Lord moved to Jagannath Puri, he arranged for Hari Das to reside nearby at Siddha Bokul. When Hari Das left his body, Mahaprabhu Himself buried him on the ocean beach and arranged for a festival of chanting to be celebrated in his honor.

In the course of His pastimes, Sri Chaitanya Mahaprabhu appointed particular individuals from His entourage to preach in the areas where they had attained the highest levels of perfection. He thus had Hari Das preach the glories of the chanting of the Holy Name by asking him a number of questions on the subject. These matters are discussed in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* and *Caitanya-bhāgavata* and other books on devotion. I began collecting Hari Das Thakur’s teachings on the Holy Name from all these sources after being asked by a certain Vaishnava to write a book on the subject.

Some distant devotee friends provided me with other materials related to Hari Das Thakur that I examined. Some of them were full of unreliable material from the Sahajiya and Baul sects. These I carefully laid aside and paid greater attention to the one or two texts that contained pure Vaishnava teachings. In one of them, I was very pleased to find a Bengali poem giving the esoteric significance of each of the sixteen names and thirty-two syllables of the Maha Mantra.It seems that Hari Das had given these explanations to a student who then composed the poem in his teacher’s name. On seeing this manuscript, I wrote a letter of profuse thanks to the devotees in Sylhet who had sent it to me.

This book, *Hari-nāma-cintāmaṇi*, contains the teachings I found in all these various source texts. I have now seen to its publication with the purpose of bringing joy to pure Vaishnavas everywhere. It was never written with anyone in mind other than the devotees who are exclusively committed to the practise of chanting the Maha Mantra. We are not prepared to enter into a debate with any others on the matter of who is qualified to read this book.

There are many different types of devotional practice, but there is only one process that is based uniquely on the chanting of the Holy Names. From the time of Lord Chaitanya Mahaprabhu, many great souls have adopted this process as it was taught by Hari Das Thakur. This is the system that was followed by the Vaishnavas of Vrindavan, and I can testify to the fact that it was also being followed in Jagannath Puri when I was there not so long ago.Sanatan Goswami and Gopal Bhatta Goswami conclude their *Hari-bhakti-vilāsa* by saying that the only spiritual practice for those who are exclusively devoted is to constantly hear, chant and remember the Holy Names without offense and without any material attachment.

*Harināma-cintāmaṇi* has been written in Bengali couplets in order to make it accessible to women, children and others who are not conversant with the Sanskrit language. For their benefit I also decided not to include any Sanskrit verses to back up any of the points that are made herein. I hope to prepare a separate volume of scriptural texts to show how everything in this book is authorized. With the blessings of Lord Krishna, this desire will be realized in the near future.

## Preface to Hari-nāma-cintāmaṇi

*His Holiness Srila Bhakti Promode Puri Maharaj*

We are happy to present this new reprint of Hari-nāma-cintāmaṇi, which was written by the most revered universal teacher, Srila Saccidananda Bhaktivinoda Thakur, along with his foreword to the first edition. Our wish is to see the victory flag of the Maha Mantra fly, for this alone will bring auspiciousness to every living being in the world. Krishna has appeared in this age in the form of His own name. He is the omnipotent Supreme Lord and has invested all His potencies in the Holy Name–nija-sarva-śaktis tatrārpitā.

Of all of the Lord’s potencies, Krishna’s power of mercy is primary. In the *Nāmāṣṭaka*, Rupa Goswami writes that the Lord’s name is even more merciful than He Himself. The Supreme Lord is a wish-fulfilling tree bestowing all fruits on His devotees. Thus His own name is appropriatedly called *cintāmaṇi,* the philosopher’s stone that miraculously fulfills all of one’s desires.

Normally, the philosopher’s stone is said to transform base metals into gold, but this is a purely material power. The Holy Name can bestow the greatest prize of all, pure ecstatic love for the Supreme Lord. It is thus sheer foolishness to pray to the Name for material benefits like wealth, adoration or prestige or anything else meant merely to staunch the cravings of the material senses. When the materially conditioned individual who has interest in things other than Krishna chants the Holy Name, he commits offenses and thus such cravings incessantly well up in his heart. Fortunately for all conditioned souls, this book has now appeared by the grace of the Supreme Lord in order to give us the means by which we may chant the Holy Names without committing these offenses and thereby be quickly liberated from all the contaminants that prevent us from experiencing prema, ecstatic love for Krishna.

**Chanting is the topmost spiritual practice**

There are innumerable devotional practices, but sixty-four of them are considered to be the best. A further analysis narrows the number of principal practices to nine, headed by hearing and chanting. All these practices are the means (or *abhidheya*) through which both the ultimate goal of spiritual life (*prayojana*) and a correct understanding of theology and metaphysics (*sambandha*) are attained. Of these nine practices, however, Sri Chaitanya Mahaprabhu singled out the chanting of the Holy Name as the topmost *abhidheya* or means of making spiritual progress.

Lord Chaitanya glorified such chanting or sankirtan in the first verse of His own *Śikṣāṣṭaka* and then went on to say that He had placed all His potencies in the Holy Name in the second verse. The glories of the Holy Name are described from the beginning to the end of the Srimad Bhagavatam, the supreme source of spiritual authority, which was revealed to Veda Vyasa as the final fruit of his spiritual realization. In various other scriptures, Narada, Brahma and Lord Shiva have each glorified the Holy Name as not only the means of spiritual perfection, but as its end as well. And one of Sri Chaitanya Mahaprabhu’s closest associates, Sanatan Goswami Prabhu, similarly glorified the Holy Name in his *Bṛhad-bhāgavatāmṛta* as follows—

*kṛṣṇasya nānā-vidha-kīrtaneṣu*  
*tan-nāma-saṅkīrtanam eva mukhyam*  
*tat-prema-sampaj-janane svayaṁ drāk*  
*śaktaṁ tataḥ śreṣṭhatamaṁ mataṁ tat*

**Of all the different types of kirtan associated with Krishna, the chanting of His holy names is considered to be chief. It alone has the power to directly bestow the treasure of love for Krishna and is thus considered to be the best of all spiritual practices. (*****Bṛhad-bhāgavatāmṛta,*** **2.3.158)**

In other words, there are many different ways one can use the tongue to glorify the Lord – by reading the various scriptures, by discussing topics related to Krishna, by singing hymns and reciting verses of praise. However, the chanting of Krishna’s names stands above all these other types of kirtan because through such chanting one quickly attains the treasure of ecstatic love for Krishna. It is thus praised throughout the scriptures as the chief, best or most powerful spiritual practice.

In this way, all of Mahaprabhu’s associates not only preached the glories of the Holy Name, but showed their commitment to constant chanting throughout their lives, giving a glowing example of its efficacy. Mahaprabhu Himself warned us, however –

*niraparādha nāma laile pāya prema dhana*

**Only one who chants the Holy Names without offenses attains the treasure of divine love. (CC 3.4.72)**

*bahu janma kare jadi śravaṇa kīrtan*  
*tabu ta nā pāya kṛṣṇa-pade prema-dhan*

**A person who commits the ten types of offenses to the Holy Name may hear and chant for many lifetimes without finding love for Krishna. (CC 1.8.16)**

Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakur wrote *Hari-nāma-cintāmaṇi* so that we would learn how to attain this supreme goal of life, love for Lord Krishna’s lotus feet, as quickly as possible through avoiding the ten offenses to the Holy Name. As the manifestation of Lord Chaitanya Mahaprabhu’s own potencies, he has shown us clearly how we can free ourselves of the ten offenses by giving us detailed descriptions of them and the ways that they disrupt our spiritual life.

In this book, Bhaktivinoda Thakur writes,

*aparādha nāhi chāṛi nāma jadi loy*  
*sahasra sādhane tāra bhakti nāhi hoy*

**Someone who chants the Holy Name without giving up the ten offenses will not attain devotion to Krishna, though engaged in spiritual practices for hundreds of years.**

He further describes “expertise in bhajan”:

*daśa aparādha chāṛi nāmera grahaṇ*  
*ihāi naipuṇya hoy sādhane bhajan*

**Expertise in the practise of spiritual life and worshiping the Lord consists in chanting the Holy Name without committing the ten offenses.**

This book thus gives not only an extensive explanation of *sambandha, abhidheya* and *prayojana*, but also of the ten offenses to the Holy Name, *nāmābhāsa* or the semblance of the Holy Name, and *śuddha-nāma* or the pure Name.

The Thakur writes, “We are obliged to spend some part of each day sleeping in order to maintain the body. We should keep this time to a minimum, however, and spend every waking moment calling out the Holy Name with sincere feeling. This will result in the destruction of the offenses—

*diva-rātra nāma loy anutāpa kare*  
*tabe aparādha jāya nāma-phala dhare*  
*aparādha gate śuddha-nāmera udoy*  
*śuddha-nāma bhāva-moy āra prema-moy*

**If you chant day and night with contrition, then all your offenses dissipate and your chanting will bear fruit. When the offenses are gone, the pure Name appears, producing first transcendental feeling for Krishna and then ecstatic love.**

**Differing versions of the ten offenses**

In this book, Bhaktivinoda Thakur cites the ten offenses described in the *Padma-purāṇa,* but interestingly gives a somewhat different enumeration than he does elsewhere, as in *Jaiva Dharma,* for instance. He combines the offense known as *artha-vāda*, “considering the descriptions of the Holy Names’ glories to be exaggeration” with *hari-nāmni kalpanaṁ,* “imagining meanings to the Holy Name.”

*hari-nāme artha-vāda kalpanā cintana*  
*pañcamāparādha prabho śrī-śacīnandana*

***Artha-vāda*****, or the tendency to imagine meanings for the Holy Name, is the fifth offense, O Lord Sachinandan.**

In *Jaiva Dharma,* the Thakur lists these two separately as the fifth and sixth offenses. He here places “sinful activity on the strength of chanting” (*nāmno balād yasya hi pāpa-buddhiḥ*) in the sixth place, “thinking that chanting the Holy Name is in any way equal to any other kind of religious activity, vow of renunciation, sacrificial ritual or auspicious mundane performance” in the seventh, and *pramāda* or “inattention” in the eighth place. In the *Jaiva Dharma* and elsewhere, the first of these is usually taken as the seventh offense, while the other two are the eighth. In the customary interpretation, *pramāda* is understood as an adjective (“erroneous” or “mistaken”) describing this offense rather than as a separate offense in its own right. Here, the Thakur describes it as an offense with three components: lethargy (*jāḍya*), distraction (*vikṣepa*) and indifference (*audāsīnya*). Though these two versions of the offenses differ somewhat, the ultimate purpose is the same: to emphasize the importance of chanting attentively and with proper understanding.

**How much should we chant?**

In chapter 12 of *Harināma-cintāmaṇi,* the Thakur writes,

*eka grantha saṅkhyā kari ārambhibe nāma*  
*krame tina lakṣa smari pūre manaskāma*  
*saṅkhyādhikye spṛhā chāṛi ekāgra mānase*  
*nirantara kari nāma tava kṛpā vaśe*

**One should start by chanting a** ***granthi*** **or four rounds of the Maha Mantra and gradually work up to chanting 300,000 Names every day. By so doing, all desires will be fulfilled. By Your mercy, I have given up the desire to chant a greater number, and simply chant constantly with focused attention.**

The Thakur himself comments on this verse, “It is more important to chant constantly with feeling, clearly hearing every syllable, than to simply increase the number of rounds one is chanting.”

Bhaktivinoda Thakur has also devoted an entire chapter in this book to the offenses in serving the Lord’s deity form.

*nāma, vigraha, svarūpa, tina eka-rūpa*  
*tine bheda nāhi tine cid-ānanda-rūpa*

**Krishna’s name, His form and His essential being are all one. They are concentrated spiritual bliss and there is no distinction to be made between them. (CC 2.17.131)**

**Bhajan pranali**

Any person desiring to progress on the path to spiritual perfection should make this little book his constant companion, for like Narottam Das, we should vow to follow the teachings of the great authorities or *mahājanas*. By closely following the directions found in *Hari-nāma-cintāmaṇi*, we are sure to make rapid advancement. Above all, it is important that we carefully read the fifteenth chapter describing the advanced state of chanting, examining all the comments Bhaktivinoda Thakur has made in his footnotes and discussing these points in the association of experienced devotees. Mahaprabhu told us that the Holy Name is both the means and end of spiritual life. Bhaktivinoda Thakur has explained elsewhere how one makes progress on the spiritual path by the grace of the Holy Name—

*īṣat vikaśi punaḥ dekhāya nija-rūpa-guṇa*  
*citta hari laya kṛṣṇa pāśa*  
*pūrṇa vikaśita hañā braje more jāya lañā*  
*dekhāya nija svarūpa vilāsa*

**When the Name is even slightly revealed, it shows me my own spiritual form and characteristics. It steals my mind and takes it to Krishna’s side. When the Name is fully revealed, it takes me directly to Vraja, where it shows me my personal role in the eternal pastimes. (*****Kṛṣṇa-nāma dhare kata bala? Śaraṇāgati*****)**

Through dedicated worship of the Holy Name, one attains all perfection through the blessings of the Name Itself. This is the Lord’s own promise.

*nāma cintāmaṇiḥ kṛṣṇaś*  
*caitanya rasa vigrahaḥ*  
*pūrṇaḥ śuddho nitya mukto’*  
*bhinnatvān nāma nāminoḥ*

**The Holy Name is a philosopher’s stone, the manifestation of conscious delight. It is complete, pure, and eternally liberated, because there is no difference between the Name and the One who is named.**

Bhaktivinoda Thakur has elaborated on this verse in his *Amṛta-pravāha-bhāṣya* to *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (2.17.133) as follows—

Krishna’s name is purely spiritual and therefore a type of spiritual touchstone. It is Krishna Himself in a form that is full of spiritual experience. It is complete, that is, it is completely unlike any material substance that is bound and limited. It is pure, that is, it is not a part of the illusory energy. And it is eternally liberated, because it is always spiritual. It is never limited by contact with matter. This is true because there is no difference between the Name and the Supreme Person who is being named.

The Holy Name is the divine touchstone. It is the most valuable jewel that gives us the intimate service to the Lord that we most desire. Therefore, it is the most suitable refuge for every living entity.

The most merciful incarnation of the Lord, the son of Sachi, the golden avatar, Lord Sri Chaitanya, taught that we especially need the Holy Name in this age of Kali, when all other means of spiritual practice are beyond our capabilities. Meditation, sacrifice and temple worship were prescribed in other ages, but only the Holy Name suffices to bring the benefits associated with these other practices in this present age. If we can dedicate ourselves fully to this Holy Name, which was glorified by Chaitanya Mahaprabhu and which He Himself fervently chanted, and if we can thus follow in the path shown by our spiritual masters and Mahaprabhu’s own associates like Svarupa Damodar and Rupa Goswami, then we will easily receive the most precious and effulgent gift of love that He came to give, a gift that had never been offered before.

*jayati jayati nāmānanda-rūpaṁ murārer*  
*viramita-nija-dharma-dhyāna-pūjādi-yatnam*  
*katham api sakṛd āttaṁ muktidaṁ prāṇināṁ yat*  
*paramam amṛtam ekaṁ jīvanaṁ bhūṣaṇaṁ me*

**All glories, all glories to Lord Krishn’s joyful form in His holy name. By chanting it, I have forgotten to pay attention to my other prescribed duties, even to my spiritual duties of meditation and deity worship. No matter how it is done, as soon as the Name touches a living being’s lips, it grants liberation. The Holy Name is my supreme ambrosia; it is my life and my unique ornament. (*****Bṛhad-bhāgavatāmṛta*** **1.1.5)**

We look forward to seeing this book being favorably received by the community of devotees.

The servant of the servants of Hari, Guru and the Vaishnavas  
Bhakti Promode Puri  
(Chief of the editorial board, *Chaitanya Vani.*)

## The Bhaktivinoda Bhajan Companion

The two books, *Harināma-cintāmaṇi* and *Bhajana-rahasya,* come from a transitional period in Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakur’s life. After retiring from service to the British government in 1894, the Thakur divided his time between Nabadwip, where he engaged in bhajan of the Holy Name at Svanandasukhada Kunj and promoted Lord Chaitanya’s birthplace in Mayapur, and Kolkata, where his family resided. In 1901, however, the Thakur returned to Jagannath Puri, which had been so significant for his devotional life in the early 1870s when he spent five years there as a deputy magistrate. It was in Puri that Bhaktivinoda intended to completely retire from all material entanglements and absorb himself fully in bhajan.

The Thakur purchased land near Hari Das Thakur’s samadhi on Puri’s oceanfront and built a small home, which he named, appropriately, Bhakti Kutir. He was accompanied by his disciple Krishna Das, who acted as his assistant. His son Bimala Prasad (Bhaktisiddhanta Saraswati Thakur) was also in Puri for much of this time, staying nearby in the Giridhari Asan of the Saptasan Math.

Though the original Bhakti Kutir building has since been demolished, a marble plaque with the following verse written by Bhaktivinoda Thakur himself still remains on the outside wall:

*gaura-prabhoḥ prema-vilāsa-bhūmau*  
*niṣkiñcano bhakti-vinoda-nāmā*  
*ko’pi sthito bhakti-kuṭīra-koṣṭhe*  
*smṛtvāniśaṁ nāma-guṇaṁ murāreḥ*

**In order to remember the names and qualities of Krishna Murari, he who is known as Bhaktivinoda has given up everything to come here to the land of Lord Gauranga’s pastimes, taking refuge in this cottage of devotion (*****bhakti-kuṭīra*****).**

While in Jagannath Puri, Bhaktivinoda Thakur was engaged in the last period of intense publishing activity of his life, during which he printed an edition of the *Padma-purāṇa,* translated Vishwanath Chakravarti’s *Saṅkalpa-kalpa-druma*, and discovered and published Jagadananda Pandit’s *Prema-vivarta.* No books, however, more closely reflect the Thakur’s determination to absorb himself fully in the bhajan of the Holy Name than *Harināma-cintāmaṇi* and *Bhajana-rahasya,* which truly reveal the heart of his inner life.

Unfortunately, the Thakur fell ill and was unable to see his desire fulfilled. He was obliged to return to Kolkata, where he lived out the rest of his days. Fortunately for the generations of devotees who owe their devotional lives to Bhaktivinoda Thakur and his descendants, the books that were born out of his sojourn in Lord Jagannath’s Holy Dham remain as a testimonial to his approach to the esoteric worship of the Holy Name. All devotees in the line of Bhaktivinoda Thakur will find inspiration to enter deeply into this aspect of Gaudiya Vaishnava mysticism in these two volumes. It is our great pleasure to make these works available in a new and authoritative translation for the pleasure of all devotees in the English-speaking world.

**About this translation**

Whenever a new version of a particular work comes out, it is natural that some rationale be given for it. The preaching of His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupada and other stalwart Gaudiya Vaishnavas has inspired numerous competent and worthy individuals to take up the study of Sanskrit and Bengali over the past fifty years. The result is that a major part of the Gaudiya Vaishnava canon has been translated into English and other languages and made available in one form or another to devotees and scholars throughout the world. Unfortunately, the quality of this work has not always been at the highest level of competence. In their enthusiasm, a great number of devotees made the fruits of their own learning available prematurely. Certainly, there has been no loss or diminution in this endeavor. Nevertheless, in view of the veneration that all devotees in Bhaktivinoda Thakur’s spiritual descendance feel for his works, it is absolutely necessary that the most accurate and effective translations be made available for their use.

The *Harināma-cintāmaṇi* has been translated at least three times previously by disciples of A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami: Sri Kushakratha Das Adhikari, Sri Bhanu Swami Maharaj, and Sri Sarvabhavana Das Adhikari. In researching my work on this volume, I procured these previous translations and examined them minutely. I would like to pay my obeisances to each of these devotees and to thank them for the insights they have provided for the preparation of this version. Anyone who makes a comparison of our work may find that there has been some borrowing: whenever I found turns of phrase that were particularly felicitous, I did not hesitate to use them. I hope that no offense will be incurred by such borrowings: let them stand as a compliment to their work.

Translations are of various types: one may use a general (or *bhāvānuvāda,* to use the Bengali term) approach or a literal (*ākṣarikānuvāda*) approach. In this version, the goal has been to be as literal as possible. This was also the approach taken by the prolific Kushakratha Dasji. Unfortunately, his version fails on the side of excessive economy and missed nuance, and is furthermore riddled with numerous errors and even outright misunderstandings of the original text.

Bhanu Swami’s work is a great improvement over this translation, but Sarvabhavana Prabhu’s work stands out as the best of the three. Sarvabhavana’s mastery of Bengali is beyond question and his facility with English makes his version an undisputable pleasure to read. Nevertheless, he has not resisted the temptation to embellish on the original text in such a way that it is sometimes difficult to tell what is of the original and what of the translator.

Bhaktivinoda Thakur highlights the important ideas in his text in two ways. On the one hand, he uses frequent headers—sometimes one header to introduce a single verse, where the verse often simply restates what is in the header. The Thakur also provides footnotes explaining many of the verses. These footnotes sometimes give elaborate explanations and sometimes merely restate the contents of the verse. In his translation, Sarvabhavana has simply merged the verses with the headers and footnotes, in order to eliminate repetition. In this edition, however, we have decided to revert to the original format, also including the transliterated Bengali verses at the request of many devotees who value being able to chant and memorize the text in the original language.

We have not given the original Bengali of Bhaktivinoda Thakur’s headings or footnotes, however. The footnotes have been treated in this volume as commentaries and placed after the relevant verses. In some cases, we felt further explanation was necessary, especially in places where previous translators went astray. This was done in consultation with senior Vaishnavas and in reference to Bhaktivinoda Thakur’s other writings, particularly *Jaiva Dharma* and *Caitanya-śikṣāmṛta*. It is sincerely hoped that “the advanced devotees” for whom Bhaktivinoda intended these two volumes will appreciate the added information.

In his introduction to *Harināma-cintāmaṇi*, Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakur says that though it was written for experienced devotees, he decided to write in simple Bengali verse without elaborate reference to Sanskrit sources in order to make it accessible to the less scholarly. In trying to reproduce these objectives, I tried to avoid using excessive Sanskrit and Bengali terminology in the translation, looking for effective and economical correspondences in English. Nevertheless, experienced devotees will no doubt already be familiar with many of these terms, like Namabhasa, Namaparadh, etc., which play an important role in the book.

Moreover, I have adopted a policy of unabashedly integrating much of this Vaishnava vocabulary into the English language, dropping the use of diacritics or italics in most cases, as we do not believe that they shall be considered foreign terms by practicing Vaishnavas. An extensive glossary has been added in which many of the newer terms have been discussed with reference to Bhaktivinoda Thakur’s other writings.

I have tried to keep the integrity of each verse, making each translation as pithy as possible, keeping each verse no longer than two lines in an attempt to make them as memorable and quotable as possible, as was obviously Bhaktivinoda’s intention in the original Bengali. Ideally, a translation in English verse would have been prepared, but any such effort would inevitably have run counter to the specific intention to remain as faithful as possible to the original. Perhaps one day an inspired devotee poet will take up this challenge.

**The bhava marg in Hari-nāma-cintāmaṇi**

The subject matter of the *Hari-nāma-cintāmaṇi* is Nama bhajan, worshiping the Holy Name. The book contains fifteen chapters: the first gives a brief summary of Vaishnava theology, the second introduces the subject of the Holy Name, distinguishing the pure Name from its facsimile (Namabhasa) and offensive chanting (Namaparadh). The next ten chapters discuss each of the ten offenses to the Holy Name, in the course of which description many details of Vaishnava philosophy and behavior are explained. The fourteenth chapter contains a brief overview of offenses incurred in worshiping the Deity and the fifteenth and final chapter contains a summary of the bhava marg (*bhāva-mārga*), the “end game,” as it were, of the practice of chanting the Holy Names.

Of the *Hari-nāma-cintāmaṇi’s* fifteen chapters, the last is certainly the one that has caused the most trouble to previous translators and the one that raises the most questions about devotional practice: the bhava marg is not widely practiced and is considered somewhat esoteric by many devotees. It is furthermore controversial, as it concerns an aspect of devotional life in which the siddhanta of Bhaktivinoda Thakur’s lineage coming through Bhaktisiddhanta Saraswati seems to differ in important ways from that found in the rest of the Gaudiya Vaishnava world as indeed His Divine Grace Bhakti Promode Puri Maharaj intimates in his Preface. As such, it is necessary for devotees who follow in the footsteps of Bhaktisiddhanta Saraswati to understand properly how he envisioned the practice of bhava marg and what is the eligibility of a devotee for it.

The Holy Name is the religious process for the age of Kali, but in this special Age of Kali, in which the Divine Couple of Vrindavan incarnate in a single body as Lord Sri Chaitanya Mahaprabhu, the Holy Name contains special powers to bestow the highest and most effulgent experience of the personal Godhead. Chanting it gives a taste of the immortal nectar for which we are always anxious, *pūrṇāmṛtāsvādanam*. The first taste of the Holy Name instills us with greed for the eternal association of the Supreme Lord. As such, it may be said that the raganuga spirit, devotion motivated by spontaneous desire rather than scriptural injunctions or rational analysis, is inherent in the Holy Name Itself. Anyone who accepts the leadership of Sri Chaitanya Mahaprabhu and Rupa Goswami is thus naturally destined for the raganuga bhakti path.

Thus, as Bhaktivinoda Thakur states in the *Harināma-cintāmaṇi* (15.21), the Holy Name is the bud of the flower of transcendental rasa. As this flower blossoms in the heart of the devotee, he or she progressively realizes the Supreme Lord, His form, qualities and pastimes, along with her own personal spiritual form and characteristics.

*īṣat vikaśi punaḥ dekhāya nija-rūpa-guṇa*  
*citta hari laya kṛṣṇa pāśa*  
*pūrṇa vikaśita hañā vraje more jāya lañā*  
*dekhāya nija svarūpa vilāsa*

**When the Name is even slightly revealed, it shows me its own spiritual form and characteristics. It steals my mind and takes it to Krishna’s side. When the Name is fully revealed, it takes me directly to Vraja, where it shows me my personal role in the eternal pastimes. (*****Kṛṣṇa-nāma dhare kata bala?*****)**

Bhakti Rakshak Sridhar Maharaj says something similar, though he speaks about the diksha mantra:

To get the mantra from a *sat guru*, a genuine guru, means to get the internal good will or real conception about the Lord. The seed of a banyan tree may be a small seed, but the great big banyan tree will come out of that seed. The will with which the particular sound is given by the guru to the disciple is all-important. We may not trace that at present, but in time, if a favorable environment is there, it will express itself and develop into something great.

Despite this faith in the unlimited power of the Krishna nama and the Krishna mantra to bestow the highest good without the aid of any other practice, Gaudiya Vaishnava acharyas like Rupa Goswami and Krishna Das Kaviraj have described the practice of raganuga bhakti, a kind of sadhana based on smaran, or remembering, in which one cultivates one’s siddha form and its service to the Divine Couple in Vrindavan.

In the *Hari-nāma-cintāmaṇi*, Bhaktivinoda Thakur has specifically used the term bhava marg to refer to this process of remembering. He does this in order to distinguish the motivations (raganuga) for service from the actual practice (bhava marg). The Thakur calls the instructions given to an aspirant in these spiritual practices bhava tattva (*bhāva-tattva*). Such instruction has two aspects, ashta kaliya lila, i.e., Krishna’s pastimes through the eight periods of the day, and ekadasa bhava, the eleven aspects of the practitioner’s spiritual identity or siddha deha. He does not, however, associate entry into bhava marg with Pancharatrika diksha. In *Caitanya-śikṣāmṛta*, he uses the term bhajan diksha to refer to the entry into bhava marg, but rather than being a formal process, he sees it as an informal exchange between the aspirant and his bhajan siksha guru.

According to Siddhanta Saraswati, the devotional path based primarily on the chanting of the Holy Name is also known as the Bhagavata marg, and it is driven by taste and spiritual attraction from the very beginning. Reason and logic on the one hand and respect for the scriptures on the other have a necessary secondary role to play, when because of the presence of anarthas one’s taste for chanting and attraction to service are weakened. Thus, Jiva Goswami speaks of mixed raganuga and vaidhi bhakti.

On the other hand, the Pancharatrika path gives priority to archan, which though mentioned in the *Hari-nāma-cintāmaṇi’*s fourteenth chapter and elsewhere throughout the Vaishnava scriptures, only plays a subsidiary role to the Bhagavata marg practices.In view of this, anyone who thinks that a person engaged in chanting the Holy Name is on the vidhi marg has a misunderstanding of Chaitanya Mahaprabhu’s intentions.

All these considerations are important for us to understand the reforms of Gaudiya Vaishnava practice instituted by Srila Prabhupada Bhaktisiddhanta Saraswati Thakur and which are now followed by all branches of the Gaudiya Math. In his book *Brāhmaṇa o Vaiṣṇava,* Saraswati states that “the Pancharatrika Vaishnava principles of medieval South India have to some extent entered within the current practices of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas. Descendants of the Gaudiya Vaishnava acharyas have become more or less attached to the path of archan, like the followers of the Pancharatras, and though they have sometimes spread subordination to Mahaprabhu in a pure form, they have more often spread a perverted form of His teachings.”

In particular, Saraswati criticized the understanding of raganuga bhakti that made it dependent on a series of formal practices specifically connected to Pancharatrika initiation. Saraswati Thakur condemns the practice of giving ekadasa bhava to devotees in whom the spiritual identity had not arisen as a result of practical realization:

In the Gaudiya Vaishnava sampradaya nowadays, some immature traders in Pancharatrika mantras present imaginary material names and forms as the goal of life and the path of perfection (*siddha-praṇālī*). In this way they gratify the minds of their disciples while disclosing their own foolishness and ignorance of the Vaishnava literatures.

In a letter to one of his disciples, Saraswati Thakur explained how he envisioned the development of the spiritual identity in the practitioner:

The ashta kaliya lila described by the Vaishnavas in Vrindavan should no doubt be highly regarded. However, these pastimes cannot be perceived in their true form by one who is still in a contaminated state. Some fortunate individuals become capable of knowing them after chanting for a long time, for it is part of the identity of the true self. But it can only be known after one is freed of mental contaminations. With the awakening of this spiritual identity, one automatically has constant cognition of his spiritual form.

Those who say that they can teach or reveal this identity are practicing a kind of deception; it cannot be done. On the other hand, if a devotee receives some inspiration after sincerely chanting for a long time, he should go to the sat guru or advanced devotees and ask for it to be confirmed and purified by them.

The spiritual identity has eleven aspects (*ekādaśa bhava*). There are many cases of unscrupulous *gurus* who artificially force-feed these designations on unqualified practitioners, but we cannot call this the mark of spiritual perfection. Those who have achieved identity perfection (*svarūpa-siddhi*) have attained such a realization through internal revelation. The spiritual master’s only involvement in these matters is to help the further advancement of a disciple. As a practitioner progresses toward spiritual perfection, all these things are revealed naturally within the heart that sincerely seeks service.

Saraswati Thakur thus follows the process described in *Hari-nāma-cintāmaṇi* (15.69-75) where Bhaktivinoda Thakur states that the disciple and guru discuss the taste and maturity of the disciple. The svarupa is never manufactured by the guru, but rather, as the disciple overcomes the major part of his anarthas, he actually experiences something positive like resolve (nishtha) for devotion to a particular form of Krishna. This resolve is the first glimmering of his siddha deha, the form to which this taste for service in a particular mood inevitably leads. At this time, the disciple approaches the guru for personal guidance in refining his conception of the various ingredients of rasa. The guru also directs the disciple toward certain lilas and discusses the prototype of the disciple’s siddha deha with him as a vantage point from which to meditate on and ultimately enter the lila. All of the guru’s directions will, of course, be based in the scriptures of the Goswamis, including the *Gaura-govindārcana-dīpikā* of Dhyana Chandra, which Bhaktivinoda Thakur has quoted in detail in works like *Jaiva Dharma* and *Caitanya-śikṣāmṛta.*

In *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, Krishnadas translates the first verse of the *Śikṣāṣṭaka* as follows:

*saṅkīrtana haite pāpa saṁsāra nāśana*  
*citta śuddhi sarva-bhakti-sādhana udgama*  
*kṛṣṇa-premodgama premāmṛta āsvādana*  
*kṛṣṇa-prāpti sevāmṛta samudre majjana*

**From sankirtan comes the destruction of sin and material entanglement, the purification of the mind and heart, as well as the arising of all the other practices of devotional life. It then leads to the awakening of love for Krishna, the relishing of the flavors of that love, and then to the attainment of Krishna, where one plunges into the nectarean ocean of service to the Lord. (CC 3.20.13-14)**

The expression *sarva-bhakti-sādhana udgama*, “the arising of all the other practices of devotional life” indicates that the Name or sankirtan does not necessarily directly final result of devotion, but that it does so incrementally by leading one to those practices and associations that will come naturally when one’s state of advancement make one ready to progress into the finer realms of Nam bhajan. To attempt practices before one is ready means inevitable disappointment.

**Anartha nivritti is necessary for the bhava marg**

According to some, anartha nivritti or self-purification is not a necessary precondition to engaging in bhava marg sadhana, because this path not based on prior accomplishments but on desire (*lobha*) alone. The fact of the matter is that unless the heart is purified, or unless one has accumulated an inordinate amount of sukriti, or causeless blessings from highly advanced devotees of Krishna, it is impossible for genuine lobha to manifest there. Therefore the Thakur clearly states in *Bhajana-rahasya*—

*adhikāra nā labhiyā siddha-deha bhāve*  
*viparyaya buddhi janme śaktira abhāve*  
*sāvadhāne krama dhara yadi siddhi cāo*  
*sādhura carita dekhi śuddha-buddhi pāo*  
*siddha-deha peye krame bhajana karile*  
*aṣṭa-kāla sevā-sukha anāyāse mile*

**If anyone tries to meditate on his spiritual body without having this qualification, his understanding will be turned topsy-turvy due to a lack of spiritual strength. If you want success in bhajan, then carefully follow the step-by-step process. Purify your intelligence by observing the activities of saintly devotees. If you engage in bhajan after attaining your spiritual body, in keeping with the progressive path, you will easily enjoy serving the Divine Couple through the eight periods of the day and night.**

Nevertheless, despite Siddhanta Saraswati’s insistence that the practitioner must purify his life and become free of anarthas, he ultimately exhorted his disciples to engage in the positive culture of the bhava marg and instructed them to hear and chant about these more elevated topics. During the Braj Mandala parikrama in 1932, Saraswati lectured his disciples:

Until now, we have not arranged for the public singing of lila kirtan. The reason for this is that it is a very confidential treasure. However, this is the ultimate object of our practice. However, lest anyone make the mistake of thinking that anartha nivritti is our only goal and that it is not necessary to engage in a positive cultivation of our true object, I have started these ashta kaliya lila kirtan performances.

I know that you are not all ready to hear such kirtan, but I want you to know that such a transcendental ideal exists within the realm of devotion, which is the reason that you must engage in anartha nivritti.

After anartha nivritti comes artha pravritti, or the real world of service to the Divine Couple. If we do not know that this is the goal, all our efforts may become transformed into impersonalism. Anyone who has been chanting the Holy Names for fifteen or twenty years should take note of this. All those who are just beginners have no need to listen to these kirtans. They will get the wrong idea of what is being sung. These kirtans are only for those who are *sevonmukha*, who are eager for service, and not for everyone.

Don’t think that the ashta kaliya lila smaran is the exclusive property of the Sahajiyas. In fact, it belongs to us. We have to recover it from all these cheaters. My Gurudeva heard all these things from Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakur. That is why he most confidentially repeated them to me again and again, in so many ways.

In the same article, while discussing Bhaktivinoda Thakur’s *Bhajana-rahasya* with some disciples, Srila Prabhupada said,

Do not let your days pass in trying only for anartha nivritti. The positive cultivation of the spiritual goal, artha pravṛitti, is also necessary. It is necessary to make efforts to eliminate the contaminants and obstacles—the anarthas—but only until one has begun artha pravritti. Once that becomes prominent, anartha nivritti becomes a secondary consideration.

Continuing to exhort his disciples to enter into the deeper aspects of the bhava marg, Srila Prabhupada said that simply becoming a scholar and instructing others is insufficient. It is necessary to go forward and become a practitioner of non-duplicitous bhajan oneself.

These instructions by Saraswati Thakur are very significant for devotees in his line who seem to believe that if one only chants, everything will be revealed and think that the positive cultivation of the bhava marg, i.e. hearing and chanting about the Lord’s pastimes and engaging in smaran is not necessary. Thus while Saraswati Thakur holds that a premature and artificially imposed spiritual identity is counterproductive, he never thought that one should expect full revelation of one’s spiritual identity simply by chanting the Holy Name without any positive cultivation of the bhava marg, or *artha-pravṛtti*.

**Is sannyas a necessary precondition for the bhava marg?**

Another source of confusion about raganuga bhakti, or bhava marg sadhana, is exactly when does a practitioner become qualified to engage in such cultivation. In the line of Bhaktisiddhanta Saraswati, the sannyas mantra is specifically related to taking shelter of the mood of the gopis, and therefore some feel that only those in the renounced order of life are eligible to follow the bhava marg. However, from Bhaktivinoda Thakur’s teachings and personal example, it does not seem that he felt one had wait to until one was a sannyasi or had completely renounced all vestiges of sexual life before meditating on his siddha svarupa.

It is evident that Bhaktivinoda Thakur himself was already engaged in following the practices of the bhava marg not long after his initiation in 1880, while he was still a householder at the height of his working life. He mentions his svarupa as Kamala Manjari in 1893 in *Gīti-mālā*, when he was still a householder, though well past his procreating years.

Furthermore, he repeatedly states that this is not a consideration. In *Caitanya-śikṣāmṛta*, for instance, he states that the disciple should stay with the spiritual master for a certain period of time when first establishing his ekadasa bhava, but that afterwards, he can go on doing his bhajan in his own home (*sva-sthāne sthita*).

Some people place inordinately high standards on the practitioner before permitting any discussion of ashta kaliya lila or bhava marg sadhana. There is little doubt that Saraswati Thakur felt strongly about premature entry into these topics, but as we have seen, he stated that it was possible when one has become free from the principal anarthas, specifically the offenses to the Holy Name. One can thus say with some safety that raganuga sadhana can begin at the stage of nishtha.

It is rather easy to show that those who feel one must be at a higher stage are wrong: certainly *bhāva* is on the stage of *sādhya-bhakti*, or the goal of devotional practice. The bhava marg still falls within the realm of devotional service in practice. Part of this is a result of a misunderstanding of the word *bhāva*, which has many meanings. These have been explained in part in the glossary at the end of this volume.

Srila Jiva Goswami clearly talks about two kinds of raganuga bhakti — *jāta-ruci* and *ajāta-ruci*. Here ruchi does not mean taste in a general sense, but to the specific stage of achievement known as ruchi. Evidently, then, one can be engaged on the bhava marg without having reached this elevated stage of devotional service in practice. Jiva there says that as long as one has not yet attained the ruchi stage, one may engage in the bhava marg practices but motivating himself with scriptural injunctions and rational arguments, as is appropriate for a vaidha bhakta. There is thus no justification for identifying raganuga with bhava bhakti and vaidhi marga with sadhana.

This is not to minimize the importance of beginning one’s devotional life by chanting free from anarthas before jumping ahead after a false analysis of one’s own capacities. Saraswati Thakur calls it spiritual laziness to try to enjoy the fruits of spiritual practice without undergoing the requisite purification.

After deliberating over the matter nicely in your heart you will find out that precocious thinking born of laziness can bear no real fruit. We are tiny jivas, traversing the path of vidhi, yet we are not inimical to raga. Talk of raga is very elevated, so it does not look particularly nice in our tiny mouths. Devotees whose life and soul is bhajan will mockingly dismiss us if they hear lowly souls like ourselves speaking on such elevated topics.

If one has no understanding of who Krishna is, then pretending to have the elevated qualifications necessary for the path of raga simply exposes his laziness. The Mahajans proclaim this at every step.

The Lord and His name are one entity. One who has conditioned perception of difference between the Name and the Named is utterly required to serve devotees accomplished in bhajan for rectification of his anarthas. The eternal associates of Sri Gaurasundar have described the kind of bhajan such devotees engage in. If we go around merely repeating such things like parrots, people will designate us as prakrita sahajiyas and thus bear down our self-assurance. But if we want to show all these prakrita sahajiyas mired in their misconceptions the real glory of raganuga bhakti, we have to learn the art of bhajan ourselves and only then try for others’ eternal benefit. So try to carefully understand what is written. Bhajan is nothing external, nor is it a means for showing off. Loudly call out the Holy Name, then the spirit of enjoyment in the form of laziness won’t be able to swallow us.

We should constantly pray to the Holy Name for His mercy. One who is still in a contaminated state should not engage in smaran of the daily cycle of the Lord’s pastimes (ashta kaliya lila). When we engage in chanting the Holy Names, we are simultaneously engaged in hearing and the opportunity for remembering is included in that. One should not engage in ashta kaliya lila smaran on false premises.

Saraswati Thakur’s final word, like Bhaktivinoda Thakur’s, is “deserve, then desire.”

There is no point in making a separate effort to artificially remember the Lord’s form, qualities and pastimes. The Lord and His name are one and the same. This will be understood clearly when the coverings in our hearts are removed. By chanting without offenses you will personally realize that all perfections come from the Holy Name.

## Guide to Pronunciation

Because of the difficulties involved in transliterating Bengali, we have followed the standard established in the books of Srila A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami and used the same correspondences of letters as in Sanskrit, which is a cognate alphabet. This is in fact a necessity, since it is the only way to maintain consistency. Where we have abandoned diacritics, we have tried to follow a kind of consensus pronunciation that is closer to north Indian regional pronunciation rather than Bengali, which is not considered standard outside of Bengal. Predominance of the north Indian pronunciation of Sanskrit in international discourse is the reason for choosing this way. It is a bit annoying to have to do it in this manner, but since the BBT started by transliterating the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* in this way, it is better for the serious student to buckle down and just learn the differences between the pronunciations and enunciate the Bengali as the authors intended and the way a native Bengali speaker pronounces it.

Unfortunately, if one does not know the differences in pronunciation between that of standard Sanskrit and current Bengali, one will certainly not pronounce the Bengali correctly.

For the most part the pronunciations of the two systems is the same, the most important differences are as follows:

(1) (…)

a. The first letter of the alphabet, the vowel *a* (…), which in general Sanskrit is pronounced like “schwa” (“uh”). In Bengali, however, it is pronounced more like *O*, or *o* in box.

b. Final *a* can be a problem. Two-syllable words generally drop the last *a*, so *haya* = *hOy*.

c. However, in certain cases, especially when used in poetry, in words with an indefinite meaning, the final *a* is pronounced with an even more distinctive and longer *M* sound (as in “go”). Thus *kakhana* = *kakhano, kOkhOnM.*

d. In compound letters, *a* is always pronounced. Dropped or almost silent after long vowel and simple consonant, e.g. *vāra vāra* = *bar bar*. *kleśa* = *klesh*. Strictly speaking, in verse this latter rule does not apply, as the vowel is counted in the number of metric syllables in the verse. This applies in the case of *haya* (*hoy*) as above.

(2) e (�)= *ay* as in English way or day. This comes frequently at the end of a word, both verbs and nouns, so be careful not to pronounce *mane* as *main* when it should be *mOn-ay*.

(3) *ai* (�) *= oi* or *oy.* So Vaishnava is pronounced Boishnob.

(4) *ya* (¯)

a. In the Bengali alphabet *ya* (¯) is distinguished from *antastha-ya* (Ḷ)by the addition of a dot. This does not exist in the Sanskrit Devanagari alphabet. At the beginning of a word, *ya* is pronounced exactly like *varga-ja* (œ).

b. In cases where *ya* (¯Í¯) is doubled, usually in Sanskrit words (e.g., *śayyā*) the sound is of a double *j*. (e.g., *shojja*). This occurs also after *ra-phalā*. Thus *kārya* in Sanskrit, in Bengali is written *kāryya*, and pronounced *kārjjo* (or just *kārjo*).

c. After a long *M*, *antastha-ya* (Ḷ)is pronounced like *w*. *hāoyā = hāwā, pāoyā = pāwā.*

*(5) va* (¬).

*a.* At the beginning of a word, *va* is invariably pronounced like *ba*. Indeed, the two letters are written the same in the Bengali alphabet. Nevertheless, in order to avoid the clash of Sanskrit and Bengali spellings of the same word in transliteration, we usually continue to use the more familiar Sanskrit spelling, e.g. Vishnu rather than Bengali pronunciation Bishnu, Vaishnava rather than Boishnob.

*b.* Similarly, *va* within a word more often that not becomes *wa*, especially when it is the second part of a conjunct, like *śva*, pronounced *shwO*. *viśvāsa* = *bishwas*. Indeed *va* in most Indian languages lies somewhere between the English w and v. Thus Bhagavān is pronounce more like Bhagawān. Sometimes one sees Bengalis transliterating this word as they pronounce it *Vogoban*. We wish to avoid this kind of unfamiliar spelling.

*(6) śa* (¶*, ṣa* (·), *sa* (¸). Bengalis tend to pronounce all the sibilants as *sha*, especially at the beginning of a word. Though many people teach this pronunciation, it can, for the most part, be safely ignored.

*(7) kṣa* (•Í·). The pronunciation *ksha* is not followed in Bengali, which tends to double the *k* and aspirate it, to give something like *kkha*. A name like Daksha will thus be pronounced DOkkhO.

Other than this, the letters are to be pronounced as in Sanskrit. This is a little complicated, I know, but it has been done primarily with a view to making the many, many Sanskrit words that are used in Bengali not look too unfamiliar for those only reading in transliteration. It is always best to sit down with a native speaker and to hear how they pronounce the words oneself. The pleasure of reading the original text will come with proper pronunciation.

The translator.

## 1 - An introduction to the glories of the Holy Name

## Intro

*gadāi gaurāṅga jaya jāhnavā jīvana*  
*sītādvaita jaya śrīvāsādi-bhakta-gaṇa*

**Перевод:**

**All glories to Gadadhar and Gauranga!**  
**All glories to Nityananda Prabhu, the life of Jahnava Devi!**  
**All glories to Sita Devi and Advaita Prabhu!**  
**All glories to Srivasa Pandit and all the devotees!**

*lavaṇa jaladhi tīre nīlācale śrī mandire*  
*dāru brahma puruṣa pradhāna \|*  
*jīve nistārite hari arcā-rūpe avatari’*  
*bhoga mokṣa karena pradāna \|\|2\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**On the shores of the ocean, in His temple on the blue hill, the Supreme Person resides in a wooden form – the Daru Brahma. Lord Hari appears in this deity form to save all living beings, bringing them both enjoyment in this world and liberation.**

*sei dhāme śrī caitanya mānave karite dhanya*  
*sannyāsī rūpete bhagavān \|*  
*kalite ye yuga dharma bujhāite tāra marma*  
*kāśī miśra ghare adhiṣṭhāna \|\|3\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Sri Chaitanya, the Supreme Lord playing the role of a monk, came to this very same holy land to bless humanity, He resided in the house of Kashi Mishra to explain the meaning of religious duty in this time, the Age of Kali.**

*nija bhakta vṛnda laye nije kalpa taru haye*  
*kṛṣṇa prema dena sarva jane \|*  
*nānā mate bhakta mukhe bhakti kathā śuni sukhe*  
*jīva śikṣā dena suyatane \|\|4\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Surrounded by His devotees, He became a wish-fulfilling tree giving love for Krishna to everyone. He listened to the devotional discourses of His devotees, carefully teaching all the world through them.**

**Комментарий:**

**Bhaktivinoda Thakur’s own notes:** Mahaprabhu thus listened to Ramananda Ray speak on subjects related to the divine love affairs of Radha and Krishna (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, Madhya 8), to Sarvabhauma speak on the process of attaining liberation (CC Madhya 6), to Rupa Goswami on the theory of rasa (CC Antya 1). And from Hari Das Thakur, He heard about the glories of the Holy Name (CC Antya 3).

*eka dina bhagavān samudre kariyā snāna*  
*śrī siddha bakule hari dāse \|*  
*mili ānandita mane jijñāsilā sa-yatane*  
*kise jīva tare anāyāse \|\|5\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**After bathing in the ocean one day, the Lord came to visit Hari Das at Siddha Bakul.**  
**After joyfully exchanging greetings, the Lord pointedly asked, “Tell me how one can most easily be liberated.”**

*prabhura caraṇa dhari’ aneka vinaya kari’*  
*galad-aśru pulaka śarīra \|*  
*hari-dāsa mahāśaya* *kāṅdite kāṅdite kaya*  
*prabhu tava līlā sugabhīra \|\|6\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Weeping and trembling, Hari Das touch the Lord’s lotus feet and awash in tears, began to speak:**  
**“O Lord, Your activities are unfathomable.”**

*āmi ati akiñcana nāhi mora vidyā dhana*  
*tava pada āmāra sambala \|*  
*e hena ayogya jane praśna kari’ akāraṇe*  
*bala prabhu habe kibā phala \|\|7\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**“I am most insignificant, with neither wealth nor learning, You, O Lord, are my only treasure.**  
**What then will be gained by needlessly asking questions of one as unworthy as I?**

*tumi kṛṣṇa svayaṁ prabho jīva uddhārite vibho*  
*navadvīpa dhāme avatāra \|*  
*kṛpā kari’ rāṅgā pāya rākha more gaura rāya*  
*tabe citta praphulla āmāra \|\|8\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**You, Lord, are Krishna Himself. You appeared in Nabadwip with all the powers needed to deliver the world.**  
**If You simply give me a place by Your merciful feet, then my mind will be filled with joy.**

*tomāra ananta nāma tavānanta guṇa grāma*  
*tava rūpa sukhera sāgara \|*  
*ananta tomāra līlā kṛpā kari’ prakāśilā*  
*tāi āsvādaye e pāmara \|\|9\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**You have unlimited names and unlimited virtues; Your beautful form is an ocean of happiness.**  
**Unlimited are Your pastimes, which You have revealed so fallen souls such as I may relish them.**

**Комментарий:**

**BVT:** The people of this world are able to relish the Lord’s spiritual names, forms, qualities and pastimes because He mercifully makes them available within the material nature. Normally the material body and senses of the conditioned soul are unable to perceive the Lord’s transcendental name, form, qualities and pastimes, because they are made of the spiritual substance—pure being or *śuddha sattva*. Nevertheless, out of His unlimited mercy, the Lord is capable of making these aspects of His own nature perceptible to the senses of the conditioned souls. Such direct manifestations of the spiritual nature are an aspect of its self-effulgent or self-revealing quality.

*cinmaya bhāskara tumi kiraṇera kaṇa āmi*  
*tumi prabhu, āmi nitya dāsa \|*  
*caraṇa pīyūṣa tava mama sukha suvaibhava*  
*tava nāmāmṛte mora āśa \|\|10\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**You are the divine spiritual sun and I but a particle of Your effulgence You are the master and I Your eternal servant. The ambrosia of Your lotus feet is my joy, my greatest treasure; my only hope lies in the nectar of Your name.**

*e mata adhama āmi ki balite jāni svāmī*  
*tabu ājñā kariba pālana \|*  
*yā balābe mora mukhe tomāre baliba sukhe*  
*doṣa guṇa nā kari gaṇana \|\|11\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**I am so lowly that I know not what to say, O Lord. Even so, I shall try to follow out Your command.**  
**As You make me, so shall I happily speak, without judging the merits or defects of my words:**

## The Krishna principle

*eka-mātra icchāmaya kṛṣṇa sarveśvara \|*  
*nitya śakti-yoge kṛṣṇa vibhu parātpara \|\|12\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**God is Krishna, who alone is completely independent in the exercise of His will. He is the Supreme Autocrat, greater than the greatest. He eternally expresses Himself through His divine potencies.**

**Комментарий:**

Krishna is God: He is a personal being, completely independent and free in the exercise of His will. He is by nature equipped with inconceivable potencies. These energies are under the control of the Lord and are therefore not independent. Krishna is the non-dual Absolute Truth; He is unlimited and all powerful. Monist philosophers, however, draw a distinction between the Lord and His attributes of will and power and thus conclude that the Absolute itself must be featureless, but in fact the supreme consciousness is omnipotent through the exercise of His various energies.

## Krishna and His energies

*kṛṣṇa-śakti kṛṣṇa haite nā haya svatantra \|*  
*yei śakti sei kṛṣṇa kahe veda-mantra \|\|13\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Krishna’s energies are never independent of Him. The Vedas say that these energies verily** ***are*** **Krishna.**

*kṛṣṇa vibhu, śakti tāṅra vaibhava svarūpa \|*  
*ananta vaibhave kṛṣṇa haya eka rūpa \|\|14\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Krishna is the supreme opulent, His energies are His opulences. Though possessing unlimited opulences, He has His own singular form.**

## Three kinds of opulence (vaibhava)

*śaktira prakāśa yei sei ta vaibhava \|*  
*vibhura vaibhava mātra haya anubhava \|\|15\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Krishna’s opulences are the manifestations of His energies. We experience only the opulences of the Lord \[not the energies themselves.\]**

*vaibhava trividha tava gaurāṅga sundara*  
*cid acit jīva tina śāstrera gocara \|\|16\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**O Lord Gaurasundara, Your opulences come in three categories— spiritual, material and individual jivas—to be understood through scripture.**

**Комментарий:**

**BVT:** These three categories of vaibhava are the conscious potency, the unconscious potency (Maya) and the individual living entities or jivas.

## The spiritual opulences (cid-vaibhava)

*ananta vaikuṇṭha ādi yata kṛṣṇa dhāma \|*  
*govinda śrī-kṛṣṇa hari ādi yata nāma \|\|17\|\|*  
*dvi-bhuja muralī-dhara ādi yata rūpa \|*  
*bhaktānanda-prada ādi guṇa aparūpa \|\|18\|\|*  
*vraja rasa-līlā navadvīpe saṅkīrtana \|*  
*ei rūpa kṛṣṇa-līlā vicitra gaṇana \|\|19\|\|*  
*e samasta cid-vaibhava aprākṛta haya \|*  
*āsiyā-o e prapañce prāpañcika naya \|\|20\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**All of Krishna abodes, such as His unlimited Vaikuntha realms,All of Krishna’s names, like Govinda, Krishna and Hari,All of His forms, such as His two-armed form playing a flute, All of His charming attributes, such as pleasing His devotees; and all His wonderful pastimes, such as the Rasa lila in Vraja and the congregational chanting of the Holy Names in Nabadwip, are manifestations of His transcendental, spiritual potency. Though they sometimes appear in this world, they are not of it.**

*cid-vyāpāra samudaya viṣṇu-tattva-sāra \|*  
*viṣṇu-pada bali’ vede gāya bāra bāra \|\|21\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**They are all spiritual affairs and have Godhead as their essence. Thus the Vedic literature repeatedly uses the words Vishnu pada to refer to them.**

**Комментарий:**

Krishna's spiritual opulences are all transformation of His chit shakti. The chit shakti is Krishna’s superior energy. The transformation of the complete chit shakti is His spiritual opulence (chit vaibhava). Krishna Himself is completely spiritual and everything connected with Him—His abodes, names, forms and all His pastimes are manifestations of this chit vaibhava. This chit vaibhava has three aspects: the sandhini power, which manifests all forms in the spiritual world; the samvit, which manifests the various types of transcendental consciousness; and finally the hladini power from which come the various emotions, relations and exchanges that produce divine joy. All these transformations of the chit shakti or Yogamaya are beyond material place, time and modality, being eternally pure and full of bliss.

## Having spiritual potencies makes Krishna the essence of the Godhead and pure being

*nāhi tāhe jaḍa-dharma māyāra vikāra \|*  
*jaḍātīta viṣṇu-tattva śuddha-sattva-sāra \|\|22\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The chit shakti is not unconscious like matter, nor is it the product of illusion; it is beyond dull matter, the essence of pure being; it is of the absolute nature of Godhead.**

*śuddha-sattva rajas-tamo-gandha-virahita \|*  
*rajas-tamo-miśra miśra-sattva suvidita \|\|23\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**It is well-known that “pure being” is without a hint of passion or darkness. whereas “mixed being” contains elements of both passion and darkness.**

**Комментарий:**

Sattva or “being” is of two kinds—pure (*śuddha*) and mixed (*miśra*). All being within the spiritual manifestation is pure, while that in the material manifestation is adulterated. In other words, the characteristics of passion (rajo-guna) and darkness (tamo-guna) are not found in pure being. Birth means the mode of passion. Since there is no rajo-guna in the spiritual manifestation, there is no birth there. Tamo-guna means death; since the spiritual manifestation never comes to an end, the tamo-guna does not exist within it, either. The living entities, who are the Supreme Lord’s differentiated parts, are pure being by nature, but when affected by nescience are adulterated by the modes of passion and ignorance. Though gods like Shiva are far superior to the ordinary jivas in many ways, they too are affected by the material nature through identification with their functions in this world. This identification makes them susceptible to an admixture of the material modes and so they are considered to be manifestations of “mixed being.” The Supreme Lord is of pure being and even when He appears in the material world through His inconceivable energy, he remains pure being and the master of Maya, the illusory potency. Maya is His maidservant.

*govinda vaikuṇṭha-nātha kāraṇodaśāyī \|*  
*garbhodaśāyī āra kṣīra-sindhu-śāyī \|\|24\|\|*  
*āra yata svāṁśa paricita avatāra \|*  
*sei saba śuddha-sattva viṣṇu-tattva-sāra \|\|25\|\|*  
*goloke vaikuṇṭhe āra kāraṇa-sāgare \|*  
*athavā e jaḍe thāke, viṣṇu-nāma dhare \|\|26\|\|*  
*praveśi e jaḍa viśva māyāra adhīśa \|*  
*viṣṇu-nāma prāpta vibhu sarva-deva īśa \|\|27\|\|*  
*māyāra īśvara māyī śuddha-sattva-maya \|*

**Перевод:**

**Govinda, the Lord of Vaikuntha, the Vishnu in the Causal Ocean, Vishnu in the womb ocean, or Vishnu lying in the milk ocean And all of the Lord’s incarnations, who are His undifferentiated portions, are of the absolute nature of the Godhead, pure essential being.**  
**Whether they appear in Goloka, Vaikuntha or the causal ocean, or even in the world, where they are all known as Vishnu, though they enter the material manifestation, they remain its master. As Vishnu, the supreme powerful lord is ruler over all the gods; He is the Lord of the Illusory Power, its controller and pure being in essence.**  
**Though any aspect of the Lord’s spiritual opulence may manifest in the material world, it never loses its transcendental character. This is the inconceivable nature of the spiritual potency, which is always pure being.**

## Mixed being

*miśra-sattva brahmā śiva ādi saba haya \|\|28\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Mixed being includes all creation, beginning with gods like Brahma and Shiva.**

## The Extent of the Spiritual Opulence

*e samasta viṣṇu-tattva āra viṣṇu-dhāma \|*  
*tava cid-vaibhava nātha tava līlā-grāma \|\|29\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**O Lord, all the different manifestations of Vishnu and His abodes are Your spiritual opulences, which it is Your pastime to expand.**

## The Material Opulences or Maya

*virajāra ei pāre yata vastu haya \|*  
*acit vaibhava tava caudda-loka-maya \|\|30\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Everything that lies on this side of the Viraja dividing line is Your material opulence, consisting of the fourteen spheres.**

*māyāra vaibhava bali bale devī-dhāma \|*  
*pañca-bhūta mano buddhi ahaṅkāra nāma \|\|31\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Some call it the abode of the Goddess, because it is Maya Devi’s realm.**

**Комментарий:**

It is composed of the five gross elements plus mind, intelligence and ego.The visible world and the living entity’s gross body are made up of the five gross elements. The mind, intelligence and ego make up the living entity’s subtle or psychic body. Since the living entity is by nature a spark of consciousness and pure being, he possesses transcendental mind, intelligence and ego, but these are distinct from the material psychic body. The subtle body bears the subtle desires and identifying characteristics of the jiva and is therefore known as the *vāsanā-deha* or *liṅga-deha.*

*e bhūrloka, bhuvarlokaāra svarga-loka \|*  
*maharloka, jana-tapa-satya-brahmaloka \|\|32\|\|*  
*atala-sutala-ādi nimna loka sāta \|*  
*māyika vaibhava tava śuna jagannātha \|\|33\|\|*  
*cid-vaibhava pūrṇa-tattva māyā chāyā tāra \|*

**Перевод:**

**The fourteen spheres consist of this earthly planet (Bhurloka),Bhuvarloka, Svarga, Maharloka, Janaloka, Tapoloka, and Satya or Brahmaloka—the seven upper spheres—and Atala, Sutala and five other lower realms are all Your illusory opulence, O Lord of the Universe! Now hear this: This is all simply the shadow reflection of Your complete, spiritual opulences.**

## The individual soul

*cid-aṇu-svarūpa jīva vaibhava prakāra \|\|34\|\|*  
*cid-dharma-vaśataḥ jīva svatantra gaṭhana \|*  
*saṅkhyāya ananta sukha tāra prayojana \|\|35\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The individual souls, essentially atoms of consciousness, are another of Your opulences.**  
**As a result of their conscious spiritual nature, the jivas have an independent makeup.**  
**Infinite in number, the jivas are motivated by the need to be happy.**

## The liberated souls

*sei sukha hetu yārā kṛṣṇere barila \|*  
*kṛṣṇa-pāriṣada mukta-rūpete rahila \|\|36\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**In seeking such happiness, those who choose Krishna become His associates and are thus forever liberated.**

## The bound or estranged soul

*yārā punaḥ nija-sukha kariyā bhāvanā \|*  
*pārśva-sthitā māyā prati karila kāmanā \|\|37\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Those, on the other hand, who think only of their personal happiness begin to desire the illusory sense objects waiting nearby at the ready.**

*sei saba nitya kṛṣṇa-bahirmukha haila \|*  
*devī-dhāme māyā-kṛta śarīra pāila \|\|38\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**These beings have become permanently estranged from Krishna and so abide in the realm of the Goddess, taking bodies fashioned by her.**

*puṇya pāpa karma cakre paḍiyā ekhana \|*  
*sthūla liṅga dehe sadā karena bhramaṇa \|\|39\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Caught up in the whirlpool of good and evil deeds, they forever wander through different gross and subtle bodies.**

*kabhu svarge uṭhe, kabhu niraye paḍiyā \|*  
*caurāśi lakṣa yoni bhoge bhramiyā bhramiyā \|\|40\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Sometimes they rise up to heaven and sometimes tumble down to hell, experiencing pleasure and pain in more than eight million different life forms.**

## Krishna nevertheless continues to show them mercy.

*tumi vibhu, tomāra vaibhava jīva haya \|*  
*dāsera maṅgala cintā tomāra niścaya \|\|41\|\|*  
*dāsa yāhā sukha māni kare anveṣaṇa \|*  
*tumi tāhā kṛpā kari kara vitaraṇa \|\|42\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**You are the Supreme Lord, and the jiva belongs to You; You always think of the welfare of Your servants. You mercifully furnish Your servant with whatever he thinks to be a true source of happiness.**

**Комментарий:**

Krishna is the eternal friend of the jiva and thus remains with him in whatever condition he finds himself. Not only that, but the Lord fulfills the jiva’s desires while he is in that conditioned state. The relation between the Supreme Lord and the individual soul is eternal and unbreakable. The Lord is the controller and the jiva is the controlled. Krishna is independent, while the jiva is dependent. Krishna makes the laws, the jiva must follow them. Krishna is the master, the jiva is His servant. Krishna rewards and punishes, while the jiva experiences the good and bad consequences of his actions.

## Mundane good deeds and religious ritual

*māyāra vaibhave ye anitya sukha cāya*  
*tomāra kṛpāya se anāyāse pāya \|\|43\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Those who seek the transitory pleasures of the illusory manifestation can easily fulfill their desires through Your grace.**

*sei sukha prāpty-upāya śubha-karma yata \|*  
*niramile dharma-yajña-yoga-homa-vrata \|\|44\|\|*  
*sei saba śubha-karma sadā jaḍa-maya \|*  
*cinmayī pravṛtti tāhe kabhu nā milaya \|\|45\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**You have thus provided the best means for obtaining material happiness: pious deeds like prescribed duties, sacrifice, self discipline, religious rituals and other observances. Even so, these pious actions are only material in nature and never give rise to the genuine spiritual impulse.**

**Комментарий:**

Prescribed duties (dharma) include one’s work, which is determined by one’s psychophysical makeup and social relations, or Varnashram. Vedic sacrifices include the Agnishtoma and others. Self-discipline refers to the yoga practices known as the eight-fold path. Other religious rituals are the fire sacrifices known as homa. The observances (vrata) are temporary religious vows such as fasts taken on particular days. Other pious activities include good works like contributions to the public welfare. Pious deeds are performed with material subtances within material time and space. Thus even though they may be carried out in the awareness of Vishnu as the Lord of the Sacrifice, they are not to be considered direct engagement in spiritual activity. Those who are devoid of such engagement are unable to distinguish between material and spiritual activities.

*tāhāra sādhane sādhya jaḍa-maya phala \|*  
*ucca loka bhoga sukha tāhāte prabala \|\|46\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The practice of such pious acts produces material results, consisting mainly of rebirth in higher spheres and increased material pleasure.**

*sei saba karma-bhoga nāhi ātma-śānti \|*  
*tāhāte prayāsa karā atiśaya bhrānti \|\|47\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Since material enjoyment never brings peace to the soul, any effort to seek them out is a stupendous error.**

*sei saba śubha karma upāya haiyā \|*  
*anitya upeya sādhe loka sukha diyā \|\|48\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**One who thinks that pious works are the means to happiness should know that their end is flickering worldly pleasure, nothing more.**

**Комментарий:**

Worldly happiness includes the comparatively longer (though still temporary) pleasures of heaven. Spiritual happiness is based on completely different principles.

## How to be delivered from this condition

*kabhu yadi sādhu-saṅge jānite se pāre \|*  
*āmi jīva kṛṣṇa-dāsa, yāya māyā pāre \|\|49\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If, through the association of the saintly, one at any time comes to know that he is a spiritual being and Krishna’s eternal servant, he transcends Maya’s illusion.**

*se virala phala mātra sukṛti-janita \|*  
*tuccha karma-kāṇḍa nāhi karile vihita \|\|50\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**This understanding is rare and comes from a special kind of merit  ritualistic religion can never produce—for so You have ordained.**

## The path of knowledge and the happiness of merging into Brahman

*āra yini māyāra yantraṇā mātra jāni \|*  
*mukti lābhe yatnavān tini hana jñānī \|\|51\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**One who comes to know that the world is nothing but suffering seeks liberation from it by becoming a philosopher.**

*se saba lokera janya tumi dayāmaya \|*  
*jñāna-kāṇḍa brahma-vidyā diyācha niścaya \|\|52\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**O merciful Lord! You have shown Your compassion to them also by giving them the philosophical scriptures that lead to spiritual knowledge.**

*sei vidyā māyāvāda kariyā āśraya \|*  
*jaḍa mukta haye brahme jīva haya laya \|\|53\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**By taking shelter of this knowledge, which is based on negating illusion, the jiva is liberated from matter and merges into the Spiritual Substance.**

## What is the Spiritual Substance?

*sei brahma tava aṅga-kānti jyotirmaya \|*  
*virajāra pāre sthita tāte haya laya \|\|54\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The Spiritual Substance into which the jnani dissolves is pure light; it is Your bodily effulgence, beyond the frontier between matter and spirit.**

*ye saba asure viṣṇu karena saṁhāra \|*  
*tāhārā-o sei brahme yāya māyā-pāra \|\|55\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**All the demons who meet their destruction at Vishnu’s hand also merge into that same same Brahman, beyond material illusion.**

## They are estranged from Krishna

*karmī jñānī ubhaye-i kṛṣṇa-bahirmukha \|*  
*kabhu nāhi āsvādaya kṛṣṇa-dāsya-sukha \|\|56\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Both the ritualists and the philosophers are estranged from Krishna and thus never taste the pleasures of serving Him.**

## The merit that turns the soul to devotion

*bhaktira unmukhī sei sukṛti pradhāna \|*  
*tāra phale jīva bhakta-sādhu-saṅga pāna \|\|57\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The principal kind of merit is that which turns one toward devotion, for through it the jiva is rewarded with the association of saintly devotees.**

**Комментарий:**

There are three kinds of merit or benefit (*sukṛti*) accrued from pious action: one leads to improved fruitive works (*karmonmukhī*), another to achievement of liberation (*jñānonmukhī*), and that which leads to faith in exclusive devotional service to the Lord (*bhakty-unmukhī*). Such merit is attained through the practice of devotional activities, even without full awareness of what one is doing.

*śraddhāvān haye kṛṣṇa-bhakta-saṅga kare \|*  
*nāme ruci, jīve dayā, bhakti-patha dhare \|\|58\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**With the growth of faith, he keeps the company of devotees and takes to the bhakti path, characterized by a taste for the Holy Name and compassion for all jivas.**

## Out of compassion for the ritualist and philosopher, Krishna provides them with indirect methods of achieving Him

*dayāra sāgara tumi jīvera īśvara \|*  
*karmī jñānī bahirmukha uddhāre tatpara \|\|59\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**O Lord, You are an ocean of mercy and the master of all jivas. You thus try to save both the ritualist and the philosopher, though they ignore You.**

*karma-pathe jñāna-pathe pathika ye jana \|*  
*tāhāra uddhāra lāgi tomāra yatana \|\|60\|\|*  
*sei sei pathikera maṅgala cintiyā \|*  
*gauṇa-bhakti patha eka rākhila kariyā \|\|61\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**You have arranged to deliver those who follow the paths of ritualistic religion and philosophical inquiry,by devising indirect paths of devotion for the ultimate benefit of these seekers.**
